A 高优先级公共卫生新闻

DRC - 操作概念 - 埃博拉病毒病 (EVD) 暴发,2026 年 10 月 - ReliefWeb

DRC - Concept of Operations - Ebola Virus Disease (EVD) Outbreak, October 2026 - ReliefWeb
新闻简报在实验室确认伊图里省的 Bundibugyo 菌株后,刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 于 2026 年 5 月 15 日正式宣布 埃博拉病毒病 (EVD) 暴发。截至 2026 年 9 月 26 日,暴发 仍集中在刚果民主共和国东部,7 个省的 63 个受影响卫生区报告了 8,067 例确诊病例和 3,901 例死亡。暴发 已扩展到伊图里、北基伍、南基伍、上韦莱、下韦莱、乔波和南乌班吉等地区,并构成重大公共卫生风险,特别是在伊图里、北基伍和上韦莱。由于存在国际传播风险,世界卫生组织 (WHO) 于 2026 年 5 月 17 日宣布 暴发 为国际关注的突发公共卫生事件 (PHEIC)。刚果民主共和国卫生部长呼吁动员技术和金融合作伙伴支持国家应对计划。然而,持续的不安全局势、人口流动和获得医疗服务的机会有限,继续使应对工作变得复杂化,并增加了受影响社区内进一步传播的风险。暴发 仍然是该地区公共卫生面临的严峻挑战。
查看新闻五要素
时间
暴发于2026年5月15日正式宣布,截至2026年9月26日,情况仍在持续。该报告的发布日期为2026年10月5日。
地点与对象
暴发 集中在刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 东部,特别是伊图里省、北基伍省、南基伍省、上韦莱省、下韦莱省、乔波省和南乌班吉省。它影响到当地居民,特别是在持续不安全和获得卫生服务机会有限的地区。
事件
在实验室确认伊图里省的 Bundibugyo 菌株后,刚果民主共和国于 2026 年 5 月 15 日正式宣布 埃博拉病毒病 (EVD) 暴发。暴发 正在进行中,并已导致确诊病例和死亡。
规模、影响与风险
截至2026年9月26日,已有8,067例确诊病例,3,901例死亡。暴发 已扩展到七个省份的 63 个受影响的卫生区。刚果民主共和国境内和邻国进一步出现 传播 的风险仍然很大。
应对、状态与不确定性
The World Health Organization (WHO) declared the 暴发 a Public Health Emergency of International Concern (PHEIC) on 17 May 2026. The DRC Minister of Health has called for the mobilisation of technical and financial partners to support the national response plan.然而,持续的不安全局势、人口流动和获得医疗服务的机会有限,继续使应对工作变得复杂化。
A high priorityPublic-health news

DRC - Concept of Operations - Ebola Virus Disease (EVD) Outbreak, October 2026 - ReliefWeb

News Brief
The Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) officially declared an Ebola Virus Disease (EVD) outbreak on 15 May 2026, following laboratory confirmation of the Bundibugyo strain in Ituri Province. As of 26 September 2026, the outbreak remains concentrated in eastern DRC, with 8,067 confirmed cases and 3,901 deaths reported across 63 affected health zones in seven provinces. The outbreak has expanded to areas such as Ituri, North Kivu, South Kivu, Haut-Uélé, Bas-Uélé, Tshopo, and Sud-Ubangi, and poses a significant public health risk, particularly in Ituri, North Kivu, and Haut-Uélé. The World Health Organization (WHO) declared the outbreak a Public Health Emergency of International Concern (PHEIC) on 17 May 2026 due to the risk of international spread. The DRC Minister of Health has called for the mobilisation of technical and financial partners to support the national response plan. However, ongoing insecurity, population movements, and limited access to health services continue to complicate response efforts and increase the risk of further spread within affected communities. The outbreak remains a critical challenge for public health in the region.
Original source text (English)

As of 26 September 2026, the Bundibugyo virus disease (BVD) outbreak remains concentrated in eastern Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), with sustained transmission across multiple provinces and 8,067 confirmed cases and 3,901 deaths reported. The outbreak has expanded to 63 affected health zones across seven provinces: Ituri, North Kivu, South Kivu, Haut-Uélé, Bas-Uélé, Tshopo, and Sud-Ubangi. Uganda was officially declared the end of outbreak in late August 2026 following completion of the 42-day surveillance period with no onward transmission detected. While regional preparedness measures remain in place, the outbreak continues to pose a significant public health risk in eastern DRC, particularly in Ituri, North Kivu, and Haut-Uélé, where active transmission persists. Insecurity, population movements, displacement, difficult terrain, and limited access to health services continue to complicate response efforts and increase the risk of further spread within affected communities. The World Health Organization (WHO) declared the Ebola outbreak a Public Health Emergency of International Concern (PHEIC) on 17 May 2026 due to the risk of international spread and the need for a coordinated international response. The outbreak poses a significant risk of further transmission both within the DRC and across neighbouring countries, particularly Uganda, Rwanda, Burundi, Central African Republic, and South Sudan. Population movements and regional connectivity may further facilitate the spread of the disease to other major urban centres, including Butembo and Kisangani. At present, no vaccine is available for the Bundibugyo strain. This marks the seventeenth Ebola outbreak recorded in the DRC. In response, the Minister of Health in DRC has called for the mobilisation of technical and financial partners to support the national response plan. Compounding the public health crisis, eastern DRC remains severely affected by ongoing insecurity, population displacement, limited infrastructure, and access constraints. Recent developments in North and South Kivu have resulted in new population movements, including both displacement and returns to areas where basic services and security conditions remain fragile. Elsewhere, including in Maniema and Tanganyika provinces, ongoing instability continues to heighten humanitarian needs and increase protection risks, particularly for women and children, including exposure to gender-based violence. Time
The outbreak was officially declared on 15 May 2026, and as of 26 September 2026, the situation remains ongoing. The publication date of the report is 5 October 2026.
Location and population
The outbreak is concentrated in eastern Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), specifically in Ituri, North Kivu, South Kivu, Haut-Uélé, Bas-Uélé, Tshopo, and Sud-Ubangi provinces. It affects the local population, particularly in areas with ongoing insecurity and limited access to health services.
Event
The DRC officially declared an Ebola Virus Disease (EVD) outbreak on 15 May 2026, following laboratory confirmation of the Bundibugyo strain in Ituri Province. The outbreak is ongoing and has resulted in confirmed cases and deaths.
Scale, impact and risk
As of 26 September 2026, there were 8,067 confirmed cases and 3,901 deaths reported. The outbreak has expanded to 63 affected health zones across seven provinces. The risk of further transmission within the DRC and to neighboring countries remains significant.
Response, status and uncertainty
The World Health Organization (WHO) declared the outbreak a Public Health Emergency of International Concern (PHEIC) on 17 May 2026. The DRC Minister of Health has called for the mobilisation of technical and financial partners to support the national response plan. However, ongoing insecurity, population movements, and limited access to health services continue to complicate response efforts.

肯尼亚确认首例输入性本迪布焦病毒病病例;世卫组织支持控制努力

Kenya confirms first imported Bundibugyo virus disease case; WHO supports control efforts
新闻简报肯尼亚于 2026 年 10 月 6 日确认首例输入性本迪布焦病毒病 (BVD) 病例,此前该患者在刚果民主共和国患病,随后经乌干达前往内罗毕。该患者是一名肯尼亚公民,在刚果民主共和国接受治疗,并于 2026 年 10 月 3 日抵达内罗毕后被隔离。尽管得到了支持性护理,该患者还是于 2026 年 10 月 5 日去世,埋葬仪式遵循了肯尼亚安全而有尊严的规定。政府已列出 28 名接触者,包括家人和卫生工作者,并正在追踪同一航班的 23 名乘客和 4 名机组人员。世卫组织正在通过检测、培训和风险沟通等方式支持肯尼亚,以防止进一步传播。自 2026 年 5 月以来,肯尼亚已筛查了超过 652,000 名旅行者,并培训了 5,000 名卫生工作者。该国处于高度戒备状态,在 27 个高风险县设立了隔离单位,并更新了 监测 工具。应对工作正在进行中,努力发现和遏制任何进一步的病例。目前情况仍在调查中,世卫组织根据当前信息建议不要实施旅行或贸易限制。

2026年10月6日(活动日期),2026年10月5日(发布日期)。
地点与对象
肯尼亚,重点关注内罗毕和高风险县。患者是一名肯尼亚公民,曾居住在刚果民主共和国,后来经乌干达前往内罗毕。
事件
肯尼亚确认首例输入性本迪布焦病毒病(BVD)病例。该患者在刚果民主共和国患病,在那里接受治疗,随后前往乌干达和内罗毕,在那里被隔离并于 2026 年 10 月 5 日死亡。该病例由肯尼亚当局报告,并得到世界卫生组织 (WHO) 的确认。
规模、影响与风险
尽管支持治疗,患者还是死亡。卫生当局列出了 28 名接触者,包括家人和卫生工作者,并正在追踪同一航班的 23 名乘客和 4 名机组人员。正在通过 监测 和遏制措施积极管理进一步蔓延的风险。

肯尼亚已在高风险入境点实施筛查,隔离患者,并正在监测接触者。世卫组织正在通过检测、培训和风险沟通等方式支持政府。响应工作仍在进行中,努力发现更多病例并预防 传播。情况仍在调查中。
A high priorityPublic-health news

Kenya confirms first imported Bundibugyo virus disease case; WHO supports control efforts

News Brief
Kenya confirmed its first imported case of Bundibugyo virus disease (BVD) on 6 October 2026, following the patient's illness in the Democratic Republic of the Congo and subsequent travel through Uganda to Nairobi. The patient, a Kenyan citizen, was treated in the DRC and later isolated in Nairobi after arriving on 3 October 2026. Despite supportive care, the patient died on 5 October 2026, and the burial followed Kenya’s safe and dignified protocol. The government has listed 28 contacts, including family members and health workers, and is tracing 23 passengers and four crew members from the same flight. WHO is supporting Kenya with testing, training, and risk communication to prevent further spread. Kenya has screened over 652,000 travelers and trained 5,000 health workers since May 2026. The country is on high alert, with isolation units established in 27 high-risk counties and updated surveillance tools. The response is ongoing, with efforts to detect and contain any further cases. The situation remains under investigation, and WHO advises against travel or trade restrictions based on current information.

WHO supports control efforts 06 Oct 2026 | News Release Journalists visit COESP and bolster the response to Mpox in Angola 05 Oct 2026 | News Release Cunene Steps Up Community Surveillance to Accelerate the Eradication of Guinea Worm 05 Oct 2026 | News Release Zambia strengthens health emergency response capacity 03 Oct 2026 | Cluster/Departmental news Staying active, engaged and thriving in older age 01 Oct 2026 | Feature Story Emergencies Data and evidence About us About WHO in the African region » About Us Contact us Governance Leadership Organizational structure Programmes and clusters Regional Director for Africa Accountability » Financial reports General Programme of Work Building a Healthier Africa, Together: A manifesto for transformation WHO Africa / Countries / Kenya / Kenya confirms first imported Bundibugyo virus disease case; WHO supports control efforts Kenya confirms first imported Bundibugyo virus disease case; WHO supports control efforts 6 October 2026 | News Release | Reading time: 4 min Kenya confirms first imported Bundibugyo virus disease case; WHO supports control efforts Nairobi—Following the confirmation by Kenyan authorities of the country's first imported case of Bundibugyo virus disease (BVD), the World Health Organization (WHO) is working closely with the Government of Kenya to intensify key control measures to prevent further spread of the virus. The government is strengthening its response coordination and case investigation, while monitoring and listing contacts for enhanced disease surveillance. At the core of the response efforts are screening at high-risk points of entry, and clear risk communication, while engaging communities to help prevent infection. These measures are rapidly being deployed to ensure the identification of cases and help contain further transmission. The patient, a Kenyan citizen, fell ill in the Democratic Republic of the Congo, where they had been living, and was treated at several health facilities there. The patient then travelled by road to Kampala, Uganda through Beni on 2 October 2026, and flew to Nairobi, arriving on 3 October 2026. On arrival, the patient was transported to a hospital in Nairobi and was quickly isolated. Samples tested positive for Bundibugyo virus at both the National Virology Reference Laboratory and the Kenya Medical Research Institute. Despite supportive care, the patient died on the night of 5 October 2026 and was buried on 6 October 2026 in line with the country’s Ebola safe and dignified burial protocol. The Government of Kenya notified WHO of the case in line with the International Health Regulations (2005) on 6 October 2026. Kenya is the fourth country to confirm BVD. The Democratic Republic of the Congo is responding to an ongoing BVD outbreak, while Uganda, where the Bundibugyo virus species was first detected in 2007, ended the latest outbreak in August 2026. Most cases in Uganda were imported from the Democratic Republic of the Congo and the remaining few were locally acquired among contacts and health workers linked to imported cases. France reported a travel-related case of BVD in June 2026. Health authorities in Kenya have so far listed 28 contacts, including family members and health workers who cared for the patient. They are also tracing 23 passengers and four crew members from the same flight, with arrangements underway for appropriate follow-up and quarantine of people assessed to be at risk. "Health emergency preparedness gives us a head start. Kenya has put important outbreak control measures in place. The priority now is to move swiftly to detect any further cases before the virus has an opportunity to spread. We're supporting the ongoing efforts to strengthen the response, and with rapid and coordinated action, we can prevent the virus from gaining a foothold and stop a potential larger outbreak," said Dr Mohamed Janabi, WHO Regional Director for Africa. Kenya has been on high alert since May 2026, when outbreaks were declared in the Democratic Republic of the Congo and Uganda. As of 6 October 2026, Kenya has screened over 652 000 travellers entering the country, tested 267 suspected samples and trained around 5000 health workers on Ebola prevention and management. WHO and partners have supported Kenya’s Ministry of Health and National Public Health Institute in these efforts. This includes Ebola simulation exercises and training a national pool of rapid response trainers, among them responders who served during the 2014–2016 West Africa outbreak. Isolation units across 27 high-risk counties in Kenya have been identified and assessed, case managers trained, and Ebola surveillance tools have been updated so that suspected cases can be quickly identified, reported and investigated. Risk communication and community engagement activities have also been strengthened through public messaging, media and community engagement, call centre support, and monitoring and responding to rumours and misinformation. WHO has also delivered about 1000 Ebola tests and 1000 personal protective equipment kits to high-risk counties in Kenya. Kenya's Ebola preparedness score, which tracks progress on key readiness measures such as surveillance, laboratory testing, isolation and treatment facilities and trained response teams, rose from 66% in May to 82% in July 2026. WHO advises against any restriction of travel to, or trade with, the Democratic Republic of the Congo, Uganda or Kenya based on the currently available information. WHO continues to closely monitor and, where necessary, verify travel and trade measures in relation to this event. WHO calls for continued support to the response in affected countries. Global solidarity, along with government-led responses with engaged communities, is the best route to ending the outbreak. Note to editors: Bundibugyo virus disease is a severe, often fatal illness. It spreads through direct contact with the blood or body fluids of an infected person, or with contaminated materials. There are currently no approved vaccines or specific treatments for Bundibugyo virus disease, but candidate products are being evaluated in clinical trials. IOM:多国 Bundibugyo 埃博拉病毒病 暴发 - 第 20 号情况报告(2026 年 9 月 21 日至 27 日) - ReliefWeb

IOM: Multi-Country Bundibugyo Ebola Virus Disease Outbreak - Situation Report No. 20 (21 - 27 September 2026) - ReliefWeb
新闻简报The IOM Situation Report No. 20, dated 21-27 September 2026, highlights a multi-country Bundibugyo Ebola Virus Disease outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC). As of 27 September, the DRC reported 8,116 confirmed cases and 3,924 deaths, with a case fatality rate of 48.3 per cent. Transmission is concentrated in Ituri (76.2 per cent of cases), North Kivu, and Haut-Uélé Provinces, indicating persistent challenges in early detection and access to care. The report notes that while daily case numbers have declined, community deaths continue, underscoring the ongoing public health threat. Response activities are ongoing in DRC and neighboring countries, including Uganda, Burundi, Rwanda, and South Sudan, with preparedness efforts also in Angola, the Central African Republic, Ethiopia, Kenya, the Republic of the Congo, Tanzania, and Zambia. These efforts focus on disease surveillance, community engagement, and cross-border coordination to mitigate risks.

时间
情况报告于2026年10月2日发布,提及的活动日期为2026年9月21日至27日。
地点与对象
暴发 集中在伊图里省和北基伍省,传播 也发生在上韦莱省。受影响的人口包括这些地区和邻国的社区。
事件
国际移民组织报告了截至 2026 年 9 月 27 日的多国本迪布焦 埃博拉病毒病 暴发 病例,刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 卫生部报告了确诊病例和死亡病例。
规模、影响与风险
As of 27 September 2026, the DRC had 8,116 confirmed cases and 3,924 deaths from Bundibugyo virus, resulting in a case fatality rate of 48.3 per cent. Transmission continues across multiple provinces, and community deaths persist, indicating ongoing public health risks.
应对、状态与不确定性
暴发 仍在调查中,刚果民主共和国、乌干达、布隆迪、卢旺达和南苏丹正在进行响应和准备活动。邻国也在继续开展防备工作,以加强疾病 监测 和跨境协调。

IOM: Multi-Country Bundibugyo Ebola Virus Disease Outbreak - Situation Report No. 20 (21 - 27 September 2026) - ReliefWeb

News Brief
The IOM Situation Report No. 20, dated 21-27 September 2026, highlights a multi-country Bundibugyo Ebola Virus Disease outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC). As of 27 September, the DRC reported 8,116 confirmed cases and 3,924 deaths, with a case fatality rate of 48.3 per cent. Transmission is concentrated in Ituri (76.2 per cent of cases), North Kivu, and Haut-Uélé Provinces, indicating persistent challenges in early detection and access to care. Search|t Updates Countries Disasters Organizations Topics Jobs Training Informing humanitarians worldwide 24/7 — a service provided by UN OCHA DR Congo + 9 more IOM: Multi-Country Bundibugyo Ebola Virus Disease Outbreak - Situation Report No. 20 (21 - 27 September 2026) Format Situation Report Source IOM Posted 2 Oct 2026 Originally published 2 Oct 2026 Origin View original Attachments Download Report (PDF | 2.15 MB) SITUATION OVERVIEW According to the Ministry of Health of the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), as of 27 September 2026, the country had reported 8,116 confirmed cases of Ebola disease caused by the Bundibugyo virus, including 3,924 deaths, resulting in a case fatality rate of 48.3 per cent. Since the beginning of the outbreak, 63 health zones across 7 provinces have reported at least 1 confirmed case. Transmission remains concentrated in Ituri and North Kivu Provinces, with Ituri accounting for 76.2 per cent of cumulative confirmed cases nationwide, underscoring its continued role as the epicentre of the outbreak. Although the daily number of new confirmed cases has declined from earlier peaks observed during the outbreak, transmission continues across multiple provinces, and the outbreak remains a significant public health threat. On 27 September, the Ministry of Health reported 49 new confirmed cases and 23 confirmed deaths, including 18 community deaths. Transmission remains concentrated in Ituri (27), North Kivu (18) and Haut-Uélé (4). The continued occurrence of community deaths and ongoing transmission across multiple provinces points to persistent challenges in early detection, referral, contact follow-up and timely access to care. As of 27 September, the national contact follow-up rate stood at 75.9 per cent, underscoring the need for sustained investment in disease surveillance, case investigation and response capacities. The outbreak continues to evolve within a complex humanitarian and mobility context. Population movements associated with trade, livelihoods, displacement, health-seeking behaviour and family ties continue within outbreak-affected areas and across international borders. Continued transmission in eastern DRC reinforces the importance of preparedness measures in neighbouring countries connected through established mobility corridors, including disease surveillance, population mobility monitoring, referral systems, infection, prevention and control (IPC) and risk communication and community engagement (RCCE). Preparedness activities across affected and at-risk countries continue to be informed by findings from population mobility mapping (PMM), flow monitoring, preparedness assessments and cross-border risk analyses. These approaches support evidencebased prioritization of preparedness investments, including disease surveillance, referral systems, RCCE and operational readiness measures at points of entry (PoEs), border communities, displacement settings and other high-risk locations. In line with the Inter-Agency Standing Committee (IASC) system-wide scale-up activation for infectious disease events, IOM continues to support outbreak response activities across affected and at-risk countries. Response and readiness activities remain ongoing in DRC, Uganda, Burundi, Rwanda and South Sudan, while preparedness efforts continue in Angola, the Central African Republic (CAR), Ethiopia, Kenya, the Republic of the Congo, the United Republic of Tanzania and Zambia to strengthen disease surveillance, operational readiness, community engagement and cross-border coordination capacities. No additional details on the number of cases, deaths, or exposures are provided in the evidence. The report highlights the importance of monitoring and responding to such cases, especially in the context of the African region's health emergency preparedness. The event is confirmed, and the response is active with no indication of uncertainty or unresolved information.

刚果民主共和国、肯尼亚、乌干达 |埃博拉本迪布焦病毒病 暴发 和欧盟应对措施 - DG ECHO 每日地图 | 2026/06/10 - 救济网

Democratic Republic of the Congo, Kenya, Uganda | Ebola Bundibugyo virus disease outbreak and EU response - DG ECHO Daily Map | 06/10/2026 - ReliefWeb
新闻简报ReliefWeb 于 2026 年 10 月 6 日发布的报告强调了刚果民主共和国、肯尼亚和乌干达的埃博拉本迪布焦病毒病 暴发。暴发 已得到确认,但证据中未提供具体病例、死亡或暴露数量。欧盟正在通过 DG ECHO 做出回应,并通过每日地图跟踪情况。暴发 被描述为全国范围内的,但没有提及明确的风险级别。回应正在进行中,状态仍在调查中。内容为部分摘要,无法提取完整的原创文章。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该报告于2026年10月6日发布,报告期为2026年8月1日至10月5日。
地点与对象
据报道,刚果民主共和国、肯尼亚和乌干达发生了 暴发 事件,涉及人群。
事件
埃博拉本迪布焦病毒病 暴发 正在受到监测,DG ECHO 的每日地图更新强调了欧盟的应对措施。该事件被确认为 暴发。
规模、影响与风险
暴发 被描述为全国范围内的,但所提供的证据中未报告具体病例数、死亡人数或暴露人数。对健康的影响没有量化,也没有明确的风险级别。
应对、状态与不确定性
欧盟正在通过DG ECHO积极应对,并通过每日地图跟踪情况。回应正在进行中,状态正在调查中。

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

肯尼亚确认首例埃博拉本迪布焦病毒病例 - BBC

Kenya confirms first case of Ebola Bundibugyo virus - BBC
新闻简报肯尼亚于 2026 年 10 月 6 日报告了首例本迪布焦 (Bundibugyo) 物种 埃博拉病毒 病例。该患者是一名在刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 生活了七年的肯尼亚公民,一个月前患病,在抵达内罗毕之前在刚果民主共和国的医院接受治疗。他于 10 月 5 日去世,并根据公共卫生协议于 10 月 6 日下葬。卫生部长亚丁·杜阿莱(Aden Duale)证实了这一病例,并表示患者的病毒检测呈阳性。已确定至少 28 名接触者,并正在进一步追踪 23 名乘客和 4 名机组人员。刚果民主共和国 暴发 始于 1976 年,已造成 8,544 例病例中的 4,114 人死亡,成为该国有记录以来最大规模的疫情。本迪布焦病毒通过与体液接触传播,目前尚无批准的 疫苗 或 治疗,尽管有四种 疫苗 正在开发中。肯尼亚加强了机场和边境检查,卫生部敦促保持警惕,防止传播。世界卫生组织于 8 月宣布刚果民主共和国已消除该疾病,但 暴发 仍在继续,北基伍省出现了新的热点,目前该省报告了近 40% 的病例。
查看新闻五要素
时间
Kenya confirmed the first case of Ebola Bundibugyo virus on 2026-10-06. The patient died on 2026-10-05 and was buried on 2026-10-06. The patient fell ill a month ago and was treated in DR Congo before arriving in Nairobi.
地点与对象
肯尼亚,特别是内罗毕,患者是肯尼亚公民,已在刚果民主共和国生活了七年。该患者曾从刚果民主共和国前往乌干达,然后前往内罗毕。
事件
肯尼亚确认首例埃博拉本迪布焦病毒病例,由卫生部长亚丁·杜阿勒 (Aden Duale) 报告。该病例确诊为该患者病毒检测呈阳性并死于该病毒。
规模、影响与风险
该患者的病例为单一确诊病例,已确定 28 名接触者,另有 23 名乘客和 4 名机组人员正在接受调查。刚果民主共和国的 暴发 已造成 8,544 例病例中的 4,114 人死亡,是该国有记录以来死亡人数最多的一次。该病毒通过接触受感染的体液传播,目前尚无批准的 疫苗 或 治疗,尽管有四种 疫苗 正在开发中。
应对、状态与不确定性
肯尼亚在机场和边境口岸加强了 监测。该患者被转移到 Covid-19 大流行期间建造的隔离设施。卫生当局正在追踪接触者并密切监测情况。世界卫生组织于 8 月宣布刚果民主共和国已摆脱该疾病,但 暴发 仍在继续,北基伍省出现了新的热点。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Kenya confirms first case of Ebola Bundibugyo virus - BBC

News Brief
Kenya has confirmed its first case of the Bundibugyo species of Ebola virus, reported on October 6, 2026. The patient, a Kenyan citizen living in the Democratic Republic of Congo (DRC) for seven years, fell ill a month ago and was treated in DRC hospitals before arriving in Nairobi. He died on October 5 and was buried on October 6 following public health protocols. Health Minister Aden Duale confirmed the case, stating the patient tested positive for the virus. At least 28 contacts have been identified, and further tracing of 23 passengers and four crew members is ongoing. The DRC outbreak, which began in 1976, has caused 4,114 deaths out of 8,544 cases, making it the largest recorded in the country. The Bundibugyo virus spreads through contact with bodily fluids and has no approved vaccine or treatment, though four vaccines are in development. Kenya has intensified airport and border checks, and the health ministry urged vigilance to prevent spread. The WHO declared DRC free of the disease in August, but the outbreak has continued, with new hotspots emerging in North Kivu province, where nearly 40% of cases are now reported.
Original source text (English)

Ireland Politics Scotland Scotland Politics Wales Wales Politics Africa Asia China India Australia Europe Latin America Middle East In Pictures BBC InDepth BBC Verify Sport Business World of Business Technology of Business NYSE Opening Bell Technology Watch Documentaries Artificial Intelligence Intelligence Revolution AI v the Mind Tech Now Health Watch Documentaries Culture Watch Documentaries Film & TV Music Art & Design Style Books Entertainment News Arts Watch Documentaries Arts in Motion Travel Watch Documentaries Destinations Africa Antarctica Asia Australia and Pacific Caribbean & Bermuda Central America Europe Middle East North America South America World’s Table Culture & Experiences Adventures The SpeciaList To the Ends of the Earth Earth Watch Documentaries Science Natural Wonders Climate Solutions Sustainable Business Green Living Audio Podcast Categories Radio Audio FAQs Video Watch Documentaries BBC Maestro Discover the World Live Live News Live Sport Documentaries Site search Home News Sport Business Technology Health Culture Arts Travel Earth Audio Video Live Documentaries Weather Newsletters Watch Live Kenya confirms its first Ebola death as outbreak spreads 10 hours ago Share Save Add as preferred on Google Basillioh RukangaNairobi AFP via Getty Images There are currently no approved vaccines or treatments for the Bundibugyo species of Ebola but some have been trialled Kenya has confirmed its first ever case of Ebola - a Kenyan citizen who has been living in the Democratic Republic of Congo for the past seven years. Health Minister Aden Duale said the patient, who fell ill a month ago, and was treated in several hospitals in DR Congo, died days after arriving in the capital, Nairobi. Duale said the patient boarded a flight on Saturday and underwent normal public screening at the main airport in Nairobi before he was transferred by a relative to a hospital in the city. Kenya's health ministry says it has since stepped up surveillance at airports and border crossings. More than 4,000 people have died from Ebola in DR Congo this year, in what is the second most deadly known outbreak of the disease. Shortly after DR Congo declared an outbreak of Ebola in May, it was also reported in Uganda, where two people died. However, the World Health Organization (WHO) declared that county free of the disease in August. Since then, all known cases of the highly infectious disease have been in DR Congo. The virus spreads through contact with infected bodily fluids such as blood or vomit. What is Ebola and why is stopping the latest outbreak so difficult? The current outbreak in DR Congo has been caused by the Bundibugyo species of Ebola, which is relatively rare, with no approved vaccine or treatment. There are however four vaccines in development, with human trials for one of them launched in July. Duale confirmed that the Kenyan national had tested positive for the Bundibugyo species. According to the minister, the patient first travelled by road from DR Congo to the Ugandan capital, Kampala, where he boarded a flight to Nairobi. Duale said that after being taken to the Nairobi Hospital, the patient was transferred to an isolation facility that was built during the Covid-19 pandemic. It was there that the patient "developed fever, chills, intense fatigue and weakness, painful swallowing, muscle pain and bleeding under the skin". "Doctors suspected a viral haemorrhagic fever based on his symptoms and travel history and collected samples for testing. The sample tested positive for Ebola Bundibugyo virus," Duale said. The patient died at 23:30 (20:30 GMT) on Monday and he was buried on Tuesday in line with public health safety protocols. At least 28 contacts, including family members and healthcare workers, have been identified, while health authorities are tracing a further 23 passengers and four crew members who travelled on the same flight. A senior health ministry official said the patient might have passed screening at the airport because the medication he was taking masked his symptoms. Kenya introduced tighter checks at airports and border posts after the outbreak was declared in DR Congo but these have now been further intensified. The health minister urged Kenyans "not to panic, as all systems are in place to mitigate the spread of the disease". He asked health workers to be "extra vigilant and maintain the highest infection prevention and control measures regardless of the condition being treated". EPA The Kenyan Red Cross Society oversaw the man's burial in Nairobi on Tuesday The WHO has recorded 4,114 deaths out of 8,544 cases in the current outbreak, making it the largest ever recorded in DR Congo, where Ebola was first discovered in 1976. Medical charity Médecins Sans Frontières (MSF) believes the current toll could be even higher because of gaps in surveillance and testing in the war-torn country. On Monday, MSF said the virus was spreading "like a megafire", with new hotspots emerging in North Kivu province, which borders Ituri where the virus began. Since early September this had "intensified dramatically" with the province now accounting for nearly 40% of all newly confirmed cases nationwide, MSF said. Eastern DR Congo has been riven by decades of conflict, with multiple rebel groups operating in the region amid ethnic tensions and fighting for political power and control of lucrative natural resources. The crisis has led to the death and displacement of millions of people, and hampered the country's ability to develop and maintain functional health care systems and infrastructure. Location and population
Kenya, specifically Nairobi, with the patient being a Kenyan citizen who had been living in the Democratic Republic of Congo for seven years. The patient had traveled from DR Congo to Uganda and then to Nairobi.
Event
Kenya confirmed its first case of Ebola Bundibugyo virus, which was reported by the Health Minister Aden Duale. The case is confirmed as the patient tested positive for the virus and died from it.
Scale, impact and risk
The patient's case is a single confirmed case, with 28 contacts identified and 23 additional passengers and four crew members under investigation. Response, status and uncertainty

Health authorities are tracing contacts and monitoring the situation closely. The WHO declared DR Congo free of the disease in August, but the outbreak has continued, with new hotspots emerging in North Kivu province.

Ebola science race: Vaccines and drugs tested against Bundibugyo virus - The Standard Media Group

News Brief
The news report highlights Kenya's first confirmed case of Ebola virus, raising concerns about the country's preparedness and potential trade barriers within the East African Community (EAC). The article emphasizes the critical need for swift action in the next 72 hours to prevent the spread of the virus. It also mentions the Africa CDC team's planned visit to Kenya to assess the situation and provide guidance. Additionally, Jambojet has pledged support for contact tracing efforts. The report underscores the importance of a coordinated response and the challenges faced in managing the outbreak. While the article discusses the implications of the case, it does not provide specific details on the number of cases, deaths, or exposures. The focus is on the broader public health and operational response rather than the scale of the outbreak. The Standard Media Group, a leading Kenyan media outlet, reports on the developments, highlighting the urgency of the situation and the need for enhanced measures to combat the virus.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

随着新热点的增加,埃博拉疾病 暴发 在北基伍省像一场大火一样蔓延 - 无国界医生

Ebola disease outbreak spreads like a megafire in North Kivu as new hotspots multiply - MSF
新闻简报文章报道,截至2026年10月6日,埃博拉病毒暴发正在刚果民主共和国(DRC)北基伍地区迅速蔓延。暴发被描述为像大火一样蔓延,表明局势规模庞大且快速发展。然而,证据没有提供具体的病例数、死亡人数或暴露人数。无国界医生正在积极响应暴发,提供医疗援助和人道援助。目前情况仍在调查中,没有包含有关检测、隔离或接触者追踪的详细信息。文章强调了无国界医生团队在这种情况下提供 治疗 和支持所面临的挑战,但它没有确认或否认 暴发 的确切规模或其目前在遏制工作方面的状态。
查看新闻五要素
时间
暴发 事件自发布日期2026-10-06起仍在持续,证据中未提供具体事件日期。
地点与对象
暴发 发生在刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 的北基伍地区。受影响人群包括该地区有感染该疾病风险的个人。
事件
据无国界医生报告,埃博拉病毒 暴发 正在北基伍省迅速蔓延。该状态被确认为持续的 暴发,没有具体确认或否认证据中的病例或死亡。
规模、影响与风险
暴发 被描述为像大火一样蔓延,表明 传播 的规模大且迅速。然而,证据中没有提供具体的病例数、死亡数或暴露人数。由于该疾病的快速传播和性质,该风险隐含很高。
应对、状态与不确定性
无国界医生正在积极响应暴发,提供医疗援助和人道援助。回应正在进行中,情况正在调查中。证据中没有提供有关测试、隔离或接触者追踪的具体细节。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Ebola disease outbreak spreads like a megafire in North Kivu as new hotspots multiply - MSF

News Brief
The article reports that an Ebola disease outbreak is spreading rapidly in North Kivu, a region in the Democratic Republic of Congo (DRC), as of October 6, 2026. The outbreak is described as spreading like a megafire, indicating a large and fast-moving situation. However, the evidence does not provide specific case counts, death counts, or exposure numbers. MSF is actively responding to the outbreak, offering medical assistance and humanitarian aid. The situation remains under investigation, and no detailed information about testing, isolation, or contact tracing is included. The article highlights the challenges faced by MSF teams in providing treatment and support in such contexts, but it does not confirm or deny the exact scale of the outbreak or its current status in terms of containment efforts.

GO TO SITE MSF Access CRASH Based in Paris, CRASH conducts and directs studies and analysis of MSF actions. They participate in internal training sessions and assessment missions in the field. GO TO SITE CRASH UREPH Based in Geneva, UREPH (or Research Unit) aims to improve the way MSF projects are implemented in the field and to participate in critical thinking on humanitarian and medical action. GO TO SITE UREPH ARHP Based in Barcelona, ARHP documents and reflects on the operational challenges and dilemmas faced by the MSF field teams. GO TO SITE ARHP MSF Analysis Based in Brussels, MSF Analysis intends to stimulate reflection and debate on humanitarian topics organised around the themes of migration, refugees, aid access, health policy and the environment in which aid operates. GO TO SITE MSF Logistique Amsterdam Procurement Unit This logistical centre in Amsterdam purchases, tests, and stores equipment including vehicles, communications material, power supplies, water-processing facilities and nutritional supplements. GO TO SITE Amsterdam Procurement Unit Brazilian Medical Unit BRAMU specialises in neglected tropical diseases, such as dengue and Chagas, and other infectious diseases. This medical unit is based in Rio de Janeiro, Brazil. The outbreak is occurring in North Kivu, a region in the Democratic Republic of Congo (DRC). The affected population includes individuals in this region who are at risk of contracting the disease.
Event
An Ebola disease outbreak is spreading rapidly in North Kivu, reported by MSF. The status is confirmed as an ongoing outbreak with no specific confirmation or denial of cases or deaths in the evidence.
Scale, impact and risk
The outbreak is described as spreading like a megafire, indicating a large and rapid scale of transmission. However, no specific case counts, death counts, or exposure numbers are provided in the evidence. The risk is implicitly high due to the rapid spread and the nature of the disease.
Response, status and uncertainty
MSF is actively responding to the outbreak, providing medical assistance and humanitarian aid. The response is ongoing, and the situation is under investigation. No specific details about testing, isolation, or contact tracing are provided in the evidence.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

肯尼亚报告首例埃博拉本迪布焦病毒病例,患者在内罗毕死亡

Kenya Reports First Case Of Ebola Bundibugyo Virus As Patient Dies In Nairobi
新闻简报肯尼亚报告首例由本迪布焦病毒引起的埃博拉病例,患者在内罗毕死亡。该人曾居住在刚果民主共和国 (DRC),并在经过乌干达坎帕拉前往肯尼亚之前在那里收到了 治疗。肯尼亚政府根据国际卫生法规于2026年10月6日向世界卫生组织(WHO)通报了情况。世卫组织总干事谭德塞在一份声明中证实了这一病例,并强调不应对受影响国家实施旅行或贸易限制。卫生当局已确认 28 名接触者,其中包括家庭成员和卫生工作者,并正在追踪同一航班上的 23 名乘客和 4 名机组人员。世卫组织正在支持肯尼亚、刚果民主共和国和乌干达的应对工作,重点是接触者追踪、监测 和筛查,以防止进一步出现 传播。目前情况仍在密切监测中,没有提供明确的风险级别或遏制状态。
查看新闻五要素
时间
肯尼亚政府于2026年10月6日向世界卫生组织通报了该病例。该患者于2026年10月5日在内罗毕死亡。
地点与对象
肯尼亚,特别是内罗毕,患者是一直居住在刚果民主共和国(DRC)的肯尼亚公民。
事件
肯尼亚报告首例由本迪布焦病毒引起的埃博拉病例,患者在内罗毕死亡。世界卫生组织 (WHO) 总干事谭德塞在其官方 X 账户上分享的一份声明中披露了这一事件。
规模、影响与风险
该病例为境外输入病例,已确定 28 名接触者,包括家庭成员和卫生工作者。当局正在追踪与该患者同机的 23 名乘客和 4 名机组人员。世界卫生组织建议不要对受影响国家实施旅行或贸易限制。
应对、状态与不确定性
世卫组织正在支持肯尼亚、刚果民主共和国和乌干达政府开展接触者追踪、监测、筛查和其他应对措施。目前正在密切监视局势,但没有提供有关风险级别或遏制状态的进一步详细信息。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Kenya Reports First Case Of Ebola Bundibugyo Virus As Patient Dies In Nairobi

News Brief
Kenya has reported its first case of Ebola caused by the Bundibugyo virus, with the patient dying in Nairobi. The individual had been living in the Democratic Republic of Congo (DRC) and received treatment there before traveling through Kampala, Uganda, to Kenya. The Kenyan government notified the World Health Organization (WHO) on October 6, 2026, in line with international health regulations. WHO Director-General Tedros Adhanom Ghebreyesus confirmed the case in a statement and emphasized that travel or trade restrictions should not be imposed on the affected countries. Health authorities have identified 28 contacts, including family members and health workers, and are tracing 23 passengers and four crew members on the same flight. The WHO is supporting response efforts in Kenya, the DRC, and Uganda, focusing on contact tracing, surveillance, and screening to prevent further transmission. The situation remains under close monitoring, with no explicit risk level or containment status provided.
Original source text (English)
Skip to main content Social Media Links Search Header Navigation News Opinion More topics Politics Sports Entertainment Education Lifestyle #NigeriaDecides Main navigation Videos Photos Documents Kenya Reports First Case Of Ebola Bundibugyo Virus As Patient Dies In Nairobi October 6, 2026 News According to Tedros, the patient fell ill while in the DRC and received treatment at several hospitals before travelling from the DRC to Nairobi, Kenya, through Kampala, Uganda. The Kenyan government has reported an imported case of Ebola caused by the Bundibugyo virus in a Kenyan citizen who had been living in the Democratic Republic of Congo (DRC). Director-General of the World Health Organization (WHO), Tedros Adhanom Ghebreyesus, disclosed this in a statement shared on his official X account. According to Tedros, the patient fell ill while in the DRC and received treatment at several hospitals before travelling from the DRC to Nairobi, Kenya, through Kampala, Uganda. He said the patient subsequently died in Nairobi. “The Government of Kenya has reported imported case of Ebola caused by Bundibugyo virus, in a Kenyan citizen who had been living in the DRC,” Tedros said. “The patient became ill in DRC and received treatment in several hospitals, then travelled from DRC to Nairobi via Kampala, Uganda. He died in Nairobi yesterday. “My condolences to his family and loved ones.” The WHO chief said the UN health agency was supporting the governments of Kenya, the DRC and Uganda in response efforts following the reported case. He said the measures include contact tracing, surveillance and screening to identify potential cases and prevent further transmission of the virus. “WHO is supporting the governments of Kenya, the DRC, and Uganda in contact tracing, surveillance, screening, and other response measures to rapidly identify potential cases and prevent further spread of the virus,” he said. Tedros said the WHO was continuing to monitor the situation closely. He also advised against imposing travel or trade restrictions on the affected countries based on the information currently available. The Kenyan government notified WHO of the case on October 6 in line with the International Health Regulations. Kenya is the fourth country to confirm BVD. The Democratic Republic of Congo is currently responding to an outbreak, while Uganda ended its latest outbreak in August 2026. France also reported a travel-related case in June 2026. Kenyan health authorities have so far listed 28 contacts, including family members and health workers who cared for the patient. Authorities are also tracing 23 passengers and four crew members who were on the same flight as the patient, with arrangements being made for follow-up and quarantine of people considered at risk. “WHO advises against any restriction of travel to, or trade with, the affected countries based on the currently available information,” he said. “We continue to closely monitor and, where necessary, verify travel and trade measures in relation to this epidemic.” The Bundibugyo virus is one of the viruses that causes Ebola disease, a severe illness that can spread through contact with infected bodily fluids. B routine priorityPublic-health news

Ebola Virus Disease Alert in the DRC - Atlas Global de Riesgo

News Brief
On October 2, 2026, the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) reported 66 new confirmed cases of Ebola virus disease, including 22 community deaths, across North Kivu, Ituri, Haut-Uele, and Tshopo regions. Ituri is the epicenter, accounting for 75.5% of the total confirmed cases. An additional 16 deaths were recorded in Ebola Treatment Centers, raising the total confirmed deaths to 38. There were 31 recoveries reported. As of this date, the DRC has accumulated 8,442 confirmed cases and 4,080 deaths. The outbreak is under active investigation, with contact tracing and isolation measures likely in place. The report is a partial summary, and the full original article body could not be extracted.

无国界医生表示,刚果无国界医生组织的一名工作人员检测出埃博拉本迪布焦病毒呈阳性,正在撤离 - CGTN

MEDECINS SANS FRONTIERES STAFF MEMBER HAS TESTED POSITIVE FOR THE EBOLA BUNDIBUGYO VIRUS IN CONGO AND IS BEING EVACUATED, MSF SAYS - CGTN
新闻简报A Médecins Sans Frontières (MSF) staff member has tested positive for the Bundibugyo virus in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) and is being evacuated, according to MSF. The Bundibugyo virus disease outbreak in the DRC is the largest ever recorded in the country, regardless of the Ebola virus species. The World Health Organization (WHO) declared the outbreak a Public Health Emergency of International Concern on 17 May 2026, highlighting the high risk in the DRC and neighboring countries, especially those sharing land borders. The overall risk remains very high in the DRC and high in neighboring countries, while the risk at the regional and global levels is considered low due to preparedness and rapid detection measures. The news report was published on 01-Oct-2026 and is based on a full original article body. 快速诊断和 疫苗 研究加强了对埃博拉致命病毒本迪布焦株 暴发 的响应

Rapid diagnostics and vaccine research strengthen response to outbreak of Ebola's Bundibugyo strain of deadly virus
新闻简报2026 年,刚果民主共和国的本迪布焦病毒 (BDBV) 暴发 促使研究人员开发快速诊断方法并评估现有扎伊尔埃博拉病毒 疫苗 的潜在交叉保护作用。这项由德国感染研究中心 (DZIF) 和国际合作者领导的研究表明,分散的实验室网络可以在传染病紧急情况下生成可靠的测试性能数据。十分之六的 疫苗 接受者表现出 BDBV 特异性中和活性,尽管其活性比针对扎伊尔埃博拉病毒的中和活性低约 3.5 至 3.6 倍。该研究强调了公共投资的必要性,以应对罕见的、高后果的病原体。证据中没有报告具体病例或死亡人数,调查结果基于实验室评估,而不是经过确认的 暴发 数据。
查看新闻五要素
时间
暴发的Bundibugyo病毒(BDBV)在2026年被报道,新闻的发布日期为2026-10-01。关于诊断测试和疫苗交叉保护的研究是在暴发期间进行的。
地点与对象
本迪布焦病毒 (BDBV) 的 暴发 发生于刚果民主共和国。该研究涉及国际实验室网络,包括德国和其他国家的实验室,并重点关注 BDBV 作为目标病原体。
事件
研究人员开发了一种本迪布焦病毒的快速诊断方法,并发现扎伊尔埃博拉病毒 疫苗 可以提供交叉保护的证据。这项研究是在刚果民主共和国的 BDBV 暴发 期间进行的,研究结果由德国感染研究中心 (DZIF) 及其合作者报告。
规模、影响与风险
刚果民主共和国的本迪布焦病毒(BDBV)的 暴发 凸显了应对罕见病原体的挑战。研究发现,十分之六的 疫苗 接受者表现出 BDBV 特异性中和活性,尽管该活性低于针对扎伊尔埃博拉病毒的中和活性。提供的证据中没有报告具体病例或死亡人数。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究旨在评估诊断测试并评估 疫苗 交叉保护。研究结果表明了应对 暴发 的潜在工具,但证据中没有报告确诊或疑似病例。这项研究是国际社会为改善严重后果疾病的准备工作的一部分。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Rapid diagnostics and vaccine research strengthen response to outbreak of Ebola's Bundibugyo strain of deadly virus

News Brief
In 2026, an outbreak of Bundibugyo virus (BDBV) in the Democratic Republic of the Congo prompted researchers to develop rapid diagnostic methods and assess the potential cross-protection of an existing Zaire ebolavirus vaccine. The study, led by the German Center for Infection Research (DZIF) and international collaborators, demonstrated that a decentralized network of laboratories could generate robust test-performance data during an infectious disease emergency. Six out of ten vaccine recipients showed BDBV-specific neutralising activity, though it was approximately 3.5 to 3.6 times lower than against Zaire ebolavirus. The research highlights the need for public investment in preparedness for rare, high-consequence pathogens. No specific case or death counts were reported in the evidence, and the findings are based on laboratory assessments rather than confirmed outbreak data.

Murphy Research news Rapid diagnostics and vaccine research strengthen response to outbreak of Ebola's Bundibugyo strain of deadly virus 01 Oct, 2026 by Alan Booth 3 min read Share on Facebook Share on LinkedIn Share on Twitter / X Share via Email Share Subscribe for newsletter updates Researchers have developed a rapid approach to validate diagnostic tests for Bundibugyo virus and found evidence that an existing Zaire ebolavirus vaccine could provide cross-protection, offering potential tools to strengthen responses to the 2026 outbreak The current outbreak of Bundibugyo virus (BDBV) in the Democratic Republic of the Congo has highlighted the difficulties that health authorities face when a rare and dangerous pathogen emerges for which dedicated diagnostic tests, vaccines and treatments are unavailable or at best limited. Two studies led by researchers from the German Center for Infection Research (DZIF) at Charité – Universitätsmedizin Berlin and the German Primate Center – Leibniz Institute for Primate Research in Göttingen – including international collaborators – have examined different aspects of the response. The research assessed how diagnostic tests could be evaluated rapidly and whether an established Ebola vaccine might offer some protection against BDBV. During the COVID-19 pandemic, researchers at Charité led by Professor Christian Drosten, a DZIF scientist, rapidly developed a polymerase chain reaction (PCR) assay for Sars-CoV-2 virus. Its validated design was made publicly available which allowed manufacturers and laboratories to reproduce it and helped to expand testing capacity. European Union legislation has since introduced more stringent requirements for diagnostic devices. Although intended to ensure test performance, these requirements can present difficulties during outbreaks when validated diagnostics are required rapidly and appropriate reference materials are scarce. The 2026 BDBV outbreak provided an opportunity to address this problem. BDBV belongs to the Ebolavirus genus and can cause severe Ebola virus disease but there is no vaccine or therapeutic treatment specifically licensed against it. On 20 May 2026, the Charité hospital admitted a patient from the USA who had contracted BDBV in the Democratic Republic of the Congo. Researchers obtained viral genetic material from a throat swab and established a reference standard against which candidate diagnostic assays could be evaluated. The team assembled an international network of nine laboratories that included DZIF members, the German National University Medicine Network (NUM), European research consortia, university hospitals and biosafety level four laboratories – the highest laboratory containment level. The network assessed four candidate PCR tests from two manufacturers. Within two weeks, the laboratories completed analytical and clinical evaluations, with no evidence of cross-reactivity. The results demonstrated that a decentralised network could rapidly generate robust test-performance data to support regulatory assessment and deployment during an infectious disease emergency. The researchers also highlighted the importance of collaboration between publicly funded laboratories and diagnostic manufacturers. Rare pathogens provide relatively little commercial incentive to develop tests that might be required only occasionally, so public investment would be required to maintain preparedness for high-consequence diseases. A second study, led by DZIF scientist Professor Stefan Pöhlmann at the German Primate Center, examined the biological characteristics of the 2026 BDBV strain and whether vaccination against Zaire ebolavirus might offer cross-protection. Researchers focused on the viral glycoprotein, a surface protein that allows ebolaviruses to enter host cells and represents an important target for neutralising antibodies. Because authentic BDBV requires biosafety level four containment, they used pseudovirus particles that carried glycoproteins from BDBV strains associated with outbreaks in 2007–08, 2012 and 2026. The experiments found no evidence that the 2026 glycoprotein allowed more efficient entry into human cells than those from earlier strains. Researchers also examined serum from ten healthy volunteers who had received the licensed recombinant vesicular stomatitis virus-Zaire ebolavirus vaccine, known as rVSV-ZEBOV. Samples were assessed before vaccination and at 28 and 180 days afterwards. After vaccination, antibodies could neutralise pseudoviruses that carried glycoproteins from all three BDBV strains. BDBV-specific neutralising activity was detected in six of the ten vaccine recipients at both post-vaccination time points, although activity was approximately 3.5 to 3.6 times lower than against Zaire ebolavirus. The results provide evidence that antibodies elicited by the licensed vaccine can recognise and inhibit BDBV in laboratory models. However, the researchers cautioned that the experiments did not demonstrate protection against BDBV infection or disease in people because the study used pseudoviruses and measured neutralisation in vitro. Taken together, the studies demonstrate how diagnostic science, virology and immunology can provide complementary tools during an infectious disease outbreak. International laboratory networks, accessible reference materials and regulatory mechanisms established before an emergency could help to reduce the interval between identification of an emerging pathogen and access to reliable diagnostics and potential preventive measures. Time
The outbreak of Bundibugyo virus (BDBV) is reported in 2026, with the publication date of the news being 2026-10-01. The research on diagnostic tests and vaccine cross-protection was conducted during the outbreak.
Location and population
The outbreak of Bundibugyo virus (BDBV) occurred in the Democratic Republic of the Congo. The research involved an international network of laboratories, including those in Germany and other countries, and focused on BDBV as the target pathogen.
Event
Researchers developed a rapid diagnostic method for Bundibugyo virus and found evidence that a Zaire ebolavirus vaccine could offer cross-protection. This research was conducted during an outbreak of BDBV in the Democratic Republic of the Congo, and the findings were reported by the German Center for Infection Research (DZIF) and its collaborators.
Scale, impact and risk
The outbreak of Bundibugyo virus (BDBV) in the Democratic Republic of the Congo highlighted the challenges of responding to rare pathogens. The research found that six out of ten vaccine recipients showed BDBV-specific neutralising activity, though it was lower than against Zaire ebolavirus. No specific case or death counts were reported in the supplied evidence.
Response, status and uncertainty
The research was conducted to evaluate diagnostic tests and assess vaccine cross-protection. The findings suggest potential tools for responding to the outbreak, but no confirmed or suspected cases were reported in the evidence. The study was part of an international effort to improve preparedness for high-consequence diseases.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

刚果埃博拉病毒 暴发 记录了 4,000 多人死亡,当局努力遏制该病毒

Congo’s Ebola outbreak records more than 4,000 deaths as authorities struggle to contain the virus
新闻简报刚果正在经历增长最快的埃博拉病毒 暴发,截至 2026 年 10 月 1 日,已有 4,000 多人死亡。暴发 由本迪布焦病毒引起,自 2026 年 5 月 15 日以来一直持续存在,给遏制带来了重大挑战。世界卫生组织对病毒迅速传播到新的卫生区、造成有限资源紧张的情况发出了警告。非洲疾病控制和预防中心指出,由于不安全和社区抵抗,确诊病例数可能会减少。本周早些时候,一名刚果政客因捍卫政府针对 暴发 的措施而遭到袭击并被杀,凸显了影响应对工作的紧张局势和不信任。尽管面临这些挑战,当局还是采取了以村庄为中心的方法来对抗这种病毒,尽管它面临一些居民的抵制,他们认为埃博拉病毒是一场骗局。目前情况仍在调查中,追踪接触者和公众合作方面仍然存在困难。
查看新闻五要素
时间
截至 2026 年 10 月,刚果埃博拉病毒 暴发 已导致 4,000 多人死亡,卫生部于 2026 年 10 月 1 日星期四发布了最新数据。暴发 于 2026 年 5 月 15 日宣布,本周早些时候发生了针对刚果政客的袭击事件。
地点与对象
暴发 正在刚果发生,特别是在北基伍省东部,重点是布滕博的埃博拉热点地区。受影响的人群包括该地区的社区,本迪布焦病毒是病原体。
事件
刚果卫生当局报告称,有 4,000 多人死于埃博拉病毒 暴发,该病毒是由本迪布焦病毒引起的。一名刚果政治家因捍卫政府针对 暴发 的措施而遭到袭击并被殴打致死,凸显了遏制工作中的紧张局势和挑战。
规模、影响与风险
暴发已导致8,300例确诊病例中的4,018例死亡,占病例的近一半。世界卫生组织对病毒迅速传播到新的卫生区表示担忧,这导致有限的资源捉襟见肘。非洲疾病控制和预防中心指出,由于不安全和社区抵制,确诊病例数可能正在下降。
应对、状态与不确定性
当局采取了以村庄为中心的方法来遏制 暴发,但由于对卫生工作者和政府的普遍不信任,努力受到阻碍。暴发 被描述为历史上增长最快的一次,目前情况仍在调查中,接触者追踪和社区合作方面仍面临挑战。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Congo’s Ebola outbreak records more than 4,000 deaths as authorities struggle to contain the virus

News Brief
Congo is experiencing its fastest-growing Ebola outbreak, with over 4,000 deaths reported as of October 1, 2026. The outbreak, caused by the Bundibugyo virus, has been ongoing since May 15, 2026, and has led to significant challenges in containment. The World Health Organization has raised alarms about the virus spreading rapidly into new health zones, straining limited resources. The Africa Centers for Disease Control and Prevention noted that the number of confirmed cases may be decreasing due to insecurity and community resistance. A Congolese politician was attacked and killed earlier this week for defending government measures against the outbreak, underscoring the tensions and mistrust affecting response efforts. Despite these challenges, authorities have initiated a village-centered approach to combat the virus, though it faces resistance from some residents who dismiss Ebola as a hoax. The situation remains under investigation, with ongoing difficulties in contact tracing and public cooperation.
Original source text (English)

The latest figures were released by the health ministry on Thursday. They show that 4,018 people have died from among 8,300 confirmed cases, making up for nearly half of the confirmed cases in what has become the fastest-growing Ebola outbreak in history. Congo announced an outbreak on May 15, caused by the rare Bundibugyo virus, which has no known treatment or vaccine. The World Health Organization has recently raised the alarm regarding the rapid geographical spread of the virus into new health zones, stretching limited resources. The Africa Centers for Disease Control and Prevention said in its latest news conference that the number of confirmed cases is falling, but that could be attributed to insecurity and challenges in getting communities to cooperate in some of the hotspots. A Congolese politician was attacked and beaten to death earlier this week for defending government measures in dealing with the outbreak, highlighting tensions that have stretched both the health community and local resources. The attack on Marie-Célestin Karondwa, a local official with Congo’s ruling Union for Democracy and Social Progress party, took place on Sunday at his home in Butembo, an Ebola hotspot in the eastern North Kivu province. His house was set on fire afterward and he died at a hospital of his injuries. Widespread mistrust of health workers and the authorities has hampered efforts to slow the Central African nation’s 17th Ebola outbreak. Authorities last month launched a village-centered approach but those efforts are battling beliefs by some residents who dismiss Ebola as a hoax. There is also deep distrust in the country’s government for reasons ranging from how it’s managed past Ebola outbreaks to chronic corruption

Location and population
The outbreak is occurring in Congo, specifically in the eastern North Kivu province, with a focus on the Ebola hotspot in Butembo. The affected population includes communities in this region, with the Bundibugyo virus being the causative agent.
Event
Congo’s health authorities reported more than 4,000 deaths from the Ebola outbreak, which is attributed to the Bundibugyo virus. A Congolese politician was attacked and beaten to death for defending government measures against the outbreak, highlighting tensions and challenges in containment efforts.

The World Health Organization has raised concerns about the rapid spread of the virus into new health zones, which has stretched limited resources. The Africa Centers for Disease Control and Prevention noted that the number of confirmed cases may be declining due to insecurity and community resistance.
Response, status and uncertainty
Authorities have launched a village-centered approach to contain the outbreak, but efforts are hindered by widespread mistrust of health workers and the government. The outbreak is described as the fastest-growing in history, and the situation remains under investigation with ongoing challenges in contact tracing and community cooperation.

Kenya records 1st Ebola death after man travels between several countries - ABC News - Breaking News, Latest News and Videos

Kenya records 1st Ebola death after man travels between several countries - ABC News - Breaking News, Latest News and Videos
新闻简报据官员称,肯尼亚出现首例埃博拉死亡病例。患者是一名肯尼亚男子,在刚果民主共和国生活了七年,大约一个月前发病,在刚果民主共和国多家医院接受治疗。他前往乌干达,然后前往内罗毕,于 2026 年 10 月 3 日上周六抵达。卫生部确认了这起死亡事件,并确定了 28 名接触者,包括家人和医护人员,同时试图联系航班上的另外 23 名乘客和 4 名机组人员。该患者的本迪布焦病毒检测呈阳性,这是埃博拉病毒的一种罕见变种,没有可用的 疫苗 或 治疗。卫生官员正在实施检疫措施并遵循安全埋葬协议,以预防社区 传播。正在进行的刚果民主共和国 暴发 的病死率为 48.2%,接触者追踪改善至 73.8%。官员敦促公众保持卫生习惯,避免与受影响地区的病人密切接触。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026 年 10 月 6 日(发布日期)。该患者大约一个月前发病,在刚果民主共和国的几家医院接受治疗。他从刚果民主共和国前往乌干达,并于上周六(10 月 12 日)抵达内罗毕。
地点与对象
肯尼亚,特别是内罗毕,患者是一名肯尼亚男子,已在刚果民主共和国(DRC)生活了七年。受影响人群包括患者的28名接触者,包括家庭成员和医护人员。
事件
肯尼亚卫生官员确认了该国首例埃博拉死亡病例。该患者被诊断患有埃博拉病毒,特别是本迪布焦病毒,这是当前刚果民主共和国 暴发 背后的埃博拉罕见变种。肯尼亚内阁卫生部长亚丁·杜阿勒和卫生部报道了这一事件。
规模、影响与风险
该患者40岁,来自刚果民主共和国西北部城市布塔。卫生部确认了 28 名接触者,并正在尝试联系与该患者同机的另外 23 名乘客和 4 名机组人员。刚果民主共和国 暴发 的病死率为 48.2%,接触者追踪已改善至 73.8%。
应对、状态与不确定性
该患者在内罗毕医院被隔离,后来被转移到东翼隔离设施。卫生官员正在追踪接触者,准备隔离安排,并遵循安全埋葬方案。卫生部强调保持高度戒备状态和预防社区 传播 的重要性。

Kenya records 1st Ebola death after man travels between several countries - ABC News - Breaking News, Latest News and Videos

News Brief
Kenya has recorded its first Ebola death, according to officials. The patient, a Kenyan man who had been living in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) for seven years, fell ill about a month ago and was treated in several hospitals in the DRC. He traveled to Uganda and then to Nairobi, arriving last Saturday, Oct. 3, 2026. The Ministry of Health confirmed the death and identified 28 contacts, including family members and healthcare workers, while attempting to reach out to 23 more passengers and four crew members on the flight. The patient tested positive for Bundibugyo virus, a rare variant of Ebola with no vaccines or treatments available. Health officials are implementing quarantine measures and following safe burial protocols to prevent community transmission. The case fatality rate for the ongoing DRC outbreak is 48.2%, with contact tracing improved to 73.8%. Officials have urged the public to maintain hygiene practices and avoid close contact with sick individuals from affected regions.

OffOn Stream on Kenya records 1st Ebola death after man travels between several countries The patient went through a health screening during his journey, officials said. ByDragana Jovanovic and Mary Kekatos October 6, 2026, 4:03 PM Kenyan health officials confirmed on Tuesday the country's first Ebola death as an outbreak in the nearby Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) continues to spread. The person was a Kenyan man who had been living in the DRC for the last seven years, Aden Duale, Kenya's cabinet secretary of health, said in a press conference. The Ministry of Health told ABC News the patient was 40 years old and traveled from Buta, a city in the northwestern DRC. Duale said the patient fell ill about a month ago and was treated in several hospitals while in the DRC. He traveled from the DRC to Kampala, the capital of neighboring Uganda, by road, then boarded a Jambo Jet flight, arriving in Kenyan capital Nairobi last Saturday, Oct. 3. Related Should I hug my children? Ebola doctor on outbreak frontlines faces daily dilemma The patient underwent a public health screening at Jomo Kenyatta International Airport and was taken by a friend to Nairobi Hospital, where he was isolated and later transferred to the East Wing Isolation Facility. Duale said the patient had symptoms including fever, chills, fatigue, weakness, muscle pain, sore throat and painful swallowing. Doctors collected a sample from the patient, which was positive for Ebola and specifically Bundibugyo virus, a rare variant of Ebola behind the current DRC outbreak for which there are no vaccines and no treatments. Kenya Health minister Aden Duale speaks to journalists as he announces the first case of Ebola virus in Kenya, Nairobi, Oct. 6, 2026. Andrew Kasuku/AP Photo Dr. Patrick Amoth, director general for health in Kenya's Ministry of Health, said during the briefing that officials believe the patient knew he was ill and reached out to friends and relatives to transport him from the airport to the hospital directly. "It is potentially possible that some of the medications that he was taking could be able to mask some of the symptoms and therefore give a false positive screening, meaning you could pass through the [airport screening system] because you have taken a medicine, say for example to lower your temperature," Amoth said. "We believe that he knew that he was unwell." Duale said the patient was given supportive treatment but passed away on Monday evening. "We take this opportunity to pass our sincere condolences to the family and friends of the departed. Arrangement for safe and dignified burial of the deceased are ongoing, and the burial is planned for later today in line with the Ebola safe and dignified public health protocols," Duale said. Duale said that 28 contacts of the patient, including family members and healthcare workers who attended to him, have been identified. Health officials are attempting to contact 23 more passengers and four crew members who were on the flight with the patient, and arrangements for quarantine among those at risk have been prepared. Related France confirms 1st Ebola case linked to DRC as UN warns outbreak is fastest-growing in Africa's history "I wish to assure the public not to panic as all systems are in place to mitigate the spread of the disease," Duale said. "The public is urged to continue with the frequent hand washing or use of alcoholic hand [wipes] and avoid close contact with sick people coming from countries reporting active transmission of Ebola disease." "You [are not] able to put a wall between Uganda and [the DRC], between Kenya and Uganda, to prevent the entry of a pathogen," he said. "Therefore, we continue to maintain a heightened state of alert, and what is important for us is to be able to pick [up] the disease when it arrives within our borders, and ensure that we prevent community transmission." People walk outside the Nairobi Hospital where the first Ebola patient had been admitted before he died in Nairobi, Kenya, October 6, 2026. Monicah Mwangi/Reuters Irene Bosire, Kenya deputy director of programs for the humanitarian nonprofit International Rescue Committee, said in a statement that with confirmation of the first imported Ebola case in Kenya, focus has shifted from preparedness to response. "The IRC is working closely with national and county governments, following Kenya's Ebola preparedness and response plan," Bosire said. "We are supporting screening activities to track the disease, trace people who have been in contact with the patient to stop it spreading, share clear information with communities, and provide supplies to prevent infection." As of Oct. 3, 8,854 cases of Ebola and 4,114 deaths have been confirmed, according to the Congo Ministry of Health, with 824 patients in isolation. The case fatality rate currently stands at 48.2% while contact tracing has improved since the summer to 73.8%, the ministry noted. 埃博拉病毒 暴发 由蝙蝠相关病毒引起,未经批准 疫苗 在刚果造成 4,000 人死亡 - 自然世界新闻

Ebola Outbreak Caused by a Bat-Linked Virus With No Approved Vaccine Passes 4,000 Deaths in Congo - Nature World News
新闻简报刚果民主共和国由本迪布焦病毒引起的埃博拉病毒 暴发 目前已导致 4,000 多人死亡,成为有记录以来增长最快的埃博拉病毒 暴发。该病毒尚未获得批准的 疫苗 或 治疗,通过与受感染的野生动物和人际接触 传播 进行传播。截至2026年9月下旬,确诊病例8,300例,全国病死率为48.4%,北基伍省为59.3%。世界卫生组织和美国疾病控制与预防中心已将美国公众面临的风险评为较低,美国没有与该 暴发 有关的病例。应对工作包括在研究环境和早期支持性护理中使用扎伊尔埃博拉病毒 疫苗,但由于不安全和社区抵制,挑战仍然存在。暴发以东部省份为中心,武装冲突和流离失所问题的遏制十分复杂。尽管出现了一些放缓的迹象,但官员们警告说,情况仍然不确定,接触者追踪仅达到预期接触者的 23% 左右。
查看新闻五要素
时间
暴发 至少自 2026 年 6 月中旬以来一直在进行,最新数据截至 2026 年 9 月下旬报告。发布日期为 2026 年 10 月 6 日。
地点与对象
暴发 以刚果民主共和国东部省份为中心,特别是北基伍省。它影响了受冲突影响社区的人口,截至 2026 年 9 月下旬报告了 8,300 例确诊病例和 4,018 例死亡。
事件
由本迪布焦病毒引起的埃博拉病毒 暴发 尚未获得批准,该病毒已在刚果民主共和国导致 4,000 多人死亡。暴发 被描述为有记录以来增长最快的病毒,该病毒通过与受感染的野生动物和人与人之间的密切接触而传播。
规模、影响与风险
截至2026年9月下旬,已有8,300例确诊病例,4,018例死亡。全国病死率为 48.4%,北基伍省病死率为 59.3%。世界卫生组织和美国疾病控制与预防中心将美国公众面临的风险评为较低,美国没有与该 暴发 相关的病例。然而,由于其高死亡率和遏制方面的挑战,暴发 仍然是一个地区紧急情况。
应对、状态与不确定性
努力包括在研究环境中使用扎伊尔埃博拉病毒 疫苗 (Ervebo),以及测试瑞德西韦和奥贝昔韦。正在使用早期支持治疗,但许多患者到达 治疗 的时间较晚。接触者追踪仅覆盖了预期接触者的 23% 左右,而且 暴发 仍在变化。非洲疾病控制和预防中心和无国界医生组织参与了应对工作,但由于不安全和社区抵制,局势仍然不确定。

Ebola Outbreak Caused by a Bat-Linked Virus With No Approved Vaccine Passes 4,000 Deaths in Congo - Nature World News

News Brief
An Ebola outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo, caused by the Bundibugyo virus, has now resulted in over 4,000 deaths, making it the fastest-growing outbreak on record. The virus, which has no approved vaccine or treatment, spreads through close contact with infected wildlife and human-to-human transmission. As of late September 2026, 8,300 cases were confirmed, with a national case fatality rate of 48.4% and 59.3% in North Kivu. The World Health Organization and U.S. Centers for Disease Control and Prevention have rated the risk to the American public as low, with no cases linked to this outbreak in the U.S. Response efforts include the use of the Zaire ebolavirus vaccine in research settings and early supportive care, though challenges persist due to insecurity and community resistance. The outbreak is centered in eastern provinces, where armed conflict and displacement have complicated containment. Despite some signs of a slowdown, officials warn that the situation remains uncertain, with contact tracing reaching only about 23% of expected contacts.
Original source text (English)
Home Animals Biology Environment Health & Medicine Tech Travel Space Natureworldnews.com Let's Connect Trending climate change Earth whales Ebola Outbreak Caused by a Bat-Linked Virus With No Approved Vaccine Passes 4,000 Deaths in Congo By Sydney Anderson Published: Oct 06 2026, 10:00 AM EDT Facebook Twitter Linkedin Comment Mail Kevin J. Olival | EcoHealth Alliance The Ebola outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo has now killed more than 4,000 people. Figures released by Congo's health ministry on Thursday, Oct. 1, show 4,018 deaths among 8,300 confirmed cases, making it the fastest-growing Ebola outbreak on record, according to CBS News and the Associated Press. What makes this outbreak unusual is the virus behind it. It is caused by the Bundibugyo virus, a rarer member of the Ebola family for which no vaccine or treatment has been approved. Existing Ebola tools were built for a different species, and doctors are testing them here without proof that they work. The World Health Organization rates the global risk as low, and the U.S. Centers for Disease Control and Prevention says the risk to Americans remains low. But the outbreak is a stark example of how a virus thought to live quietly in wildlife can become a regional emergency when it reaches crowded, conflict-hit communities. A Different Ebola Virus With Fewer Tools Ebola disease is caused by several related viruses named for where they were found. The best known is Zaire ebolavirus, which drove the West African epidemic of 2014 to 2016. For Zaire virus, there is a licensed vaccine, Ervebo, and approved antibody treatments. None of those approvals cover Bundibugyo — not the vaccine and not the antibody drugs. Doctors Without Borders notes that the two monoclonal antibody drugs approved after trials in Congo between 2018 and 2020 are specific to Zaire virus and are not effective or approved for this one. Antiviral and antibody candidates exist, but their efficacy has not been proven in clinical trials. That is why Ervebo is being used here only within research. As of late September, 6,300 health care workers had received the vaccine as part of a clinical trial, and another 1,066 people had also been vaccinated, according to an Africa Centres for Disease Control and Prevention briefing reported by CIDRAP. The vaccine works against Zaire virus, and its effectiveness against Bundibugyo is still being estimated. Congo is also studying remdesivir as a treatment and obeldesivir to prevent infection after exposure. Without a targeted drug, care relies on basics — fluids, oxygen and close monitoring. Early supportive care can save lives, but many patients reach treatment late. A Death Rate Near One in Two The national case fatality rate stands at 48.4%, according to Africa CDC data cited by CIDRAP. In North Kivu province, it is higher, at 59.3%. Those figures are consistent with what is known about this virus. The World Health Organization reports that the only two earlier Bundibugyo outbreaks, in Uganda in 2007 and Congo in 2012, had fatality rates of 30% and 50%. Bundibugyo disease begins with fever, fatigue, muscle pain, headache, and sore throat, which look like malaria or other common illnesses. That makes early detection hard, and patients are not infectious until symptoms begin. Conditions on the ground make everything harder. The outbreak is centered in eastern provinces with years of armed conflict and displacement. On Sept. 27, a ruling-party official was beaten to death in Butembo after urging Ebola prevention on the radio, and days later soldiers searching for weapons burned a camp near Bunia that held an Ebola transit center, forcing 19,000 people to flee, CIDRAP reported. Unpaid health workers have protested and gone on strike. At least 50 health workers have died. Signs of a Slowdown, With Caveats There are hints of progress. The 278 infections confirmed near the end of September were the lowest weekly count since mid-June, and weekly deaths fell to 192 from a peak of 364 in mid-August. More than 21 days have passed without a new case in 18 health zones. Officials warn those numbers may be misleading. Wessam Mankoula, an epidemiologist with Africa CDC, said at the briefing that "this curve doesn't mean that the outbreak is totally under control." Insecurity and community resistance in several hotspots have kept teams from doing surveillance, he said, and contact tracing reaches only about 23% of the expected contacts. The outbreak is also shifting. North Kivu now accounts for nearly 40% of newly confirmed cases, up from 24% at the end of August, according to Doctors Without Borders. A staff member with the group tested positive on Sept. 29 and was flown to the Netherlands for care. The Wildlife Link and What It Means Beyond Congo Bundibugyo is a zoonotic virus, meaning it originated in animals. WHO says fruit bats are suspected to be its natural reservoir. People are thought to become infected through close contact with the blood or body fluids of infected wildlife, such as bats or nonhuman primates, after which the virus spreads between people through bodily fluids, unsafe burials, and gaps in hospital infection control. The sources reviewed for this report do not identify the specific animal source of the 2026 outbreak. For U.S. readers, the CDC says the overall risk to the American public remains low, and the likelihood of Ebola spreading to the United States is very low. No cases linked to this outbreak have been reported in the country. The agency began a public health emergency response on May 17, and WHO, in its June 6 assessment, rated the global risk as low. A case diagnosed in France in June recovered without spreading the virus, and Uganda declared its own outbreak over in July. Travel rules are strict. According to the CDC's Ebola situation page, updated Oct. 1, travelers who have been in Congo within 21 days of their flight are not allowed to board commercial flights to the United States, and U.S. citizens are told to stay outside Congo for 21 days before entering the country. Travelers from Uganda and South Sudan must enter through designated airports for enhanced screening. The CDC advises against all travel to Ituri and North Kivu provinces. The next key data will come from WHO situation reports and Africa CDC briefings. Researchers are also waiting on results from the vaccine and treatment trials, which could shape how future Bundibugyo outbreaks are fought. Until then, the response depends on old tools: finding cases, isolating patients, tracing contacts and earning community trust. Related 'Deadlier than Ebola': UN Demands for Stronger Response on Measles in Congo WHO: Ignorance, Mistrust Risk Ebola Resurgence in Congo Ebola Vaccine is Coming Out Soon What Readers Want to Know How many people have died in Congo's Ebola outbreak? Congo's health ministry reported 4,018 deaths among 8,300 confirmed cases in figures released on Thursday, Oct. 1. What is Bundibugyo virus? It is one of several viruses that cause Ebola disease. It was first identified in Uganda in 2007, and this is only the third known outbreak it has caused. Is there a vaccine for this Ebola outbreak? No vaccine is approved for the Bundibugyo virus. The Ervebo vaccine, approved for a different Ebola species, is being tested in a clinical trial, mainly among health workers. Why is the death rate so high? About 48% of confirmed cases have died nationally. There is no targeted treatment, early symptoms resemble common illnesses, and conflict delays care and testing. Where does the virus come from? WHO says fruit bats are suspected to be the natural reservoir. Humans can be infected through contact with infected wildlife, then spread it to others through bodily fluids. Is the outbreak slowing down? Weekly confirmed cases and deaths have fallen from their peaks, but Africa CDC warns underreporting in insecure areas may hide ongoing transmission. What does this mean for Americans? The CDC says the risk to the American public is low, and no U.S. cases have been reported. Travelers who were in Congo within the past 21 days cannot board commercial flights to the United States, and the agency advises against all travel to Ituri and North Kivu provinces. The outbreak has been ongoing since at least mid-June 2026, with the latest figures reported as of late September 2026. The publication date is October 6, 2026.
Location and population

Event
An Ebola outbreak caused by the Bundibugyo virus, which has no approved vaccine or treatment, has resulted in over 4,000 deaths in the Democratic Republic of the Congo. The outbreak is described as the fastest-growing on record, with the virus spreading through close contact with infected wildlife and human-to-human transmission.
Scale, impact and risk
As of late September 2026, there were 8,300 confirmed cases and 4,018 deaths. The case fatality rate is 48.4% nationally and 59.3% in North Kivu. The World Health Organization and U.S. Centers for Disease Control and Prevention rate the risk to the American public as low, with no cases linked to this outbreak in the U.S. However, the outbreak remains a regional emergency due to its high fatality rate and challenges in containment.
Response, status and uncertainty
Efforts include the use of the Zaire ebolavirus vaccine (Ervebo) in research settings, as well as testing of remdesivir and obeldesivir. Early supportive care is being used, but many patients reach treatment late. Contact tracing reaches only about 23% of expected contacts, and the outbreak is still shifting. The Africa Centres for Disease Control and Prevention and Doctors Without Borders are involved in response efforts, but the situation remains uncertain due to insecurity and community resistance.

埃博拉 暴发 表明世界尚未为下一次大流行做好准备 - 日内瓦解决方案

Ebola outbreak shows that the world is not ready for the next pandemic - Geneva Solutions
新闻简报The article discusses the ongoing Ebola outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), specifically the Bundibugyo strain, which has been active since mid-May 2026. The World Health Organization (WHO) reported a decline in cases in Ituri Province but a significant increase in North Kivu Province, indicating the outbreak is evolving across multiple areas. Over 3,700 people have died since the outbreak began, with a case fatality rate of 22 per cent, the lowest ever recorded for the virus. The response includes community-based efforts to identify suspected cases and address misinformation, but challenges remain due to the humanitarian crisis and lack of resources. The article highlights the need for global readiness and funding to combat the outbreak effectively, as the DRC struggles with its response in contrast to Rwanda's successful handling of a Marburg virus outbreak in 2024. The article is a syndicated summary, and the full original article body could not be extracted.

时间
文章发表于2026-10-05,更新于2026-10-06。刚果民主共和国的埃博拉病毒暴发自2026年5月中旬以来一直在持续,最新数据为2026年8月31日至9月20日。
地点与对象
刚果民主共和国 (DRC),特别是东部地区,包括伊图里省和北基伍省。受影响的人口包括社区和农村地区,其中儿童尤其脆弱。
事件
据报道,刚果民主共和国出现了埃博拉 暴发,特别是 Bundibugyo 菌株。暴发 自 2026 年 5 月中旬以来一直在进行,世界卫生组织注意到伊图里省的病例有所下降,但北基伍省的病例显着增加。该事件已得到世界卫生组织和其他官方消息来源的证实。
规模、影响与风险
Approximately 3,700 people have died since the outbreak was declared in mid-May 2026. The case fatality rate for the outbreak stayed at 22 per cent, the lowest ever recorded for the virus. The outbreak is evolving across multiple provinces, and the risk of further transmission remains high due to community practices and misinformation.
应对、状态与不确定性
刚果民主共和国正在努力将应对工作转移到流行病学趋势最令人担忧的农村地区。接触者追踪、大规模测试和 监测 正在进行中。目前正在调查应对措施,并强调需要额外资源。该文章是联合摘要,无法提取完整的原始文章正文。

The response includes community-based efforts to identify suspected cases and address misinformation, but challenges remain due to the humanitarian crisis and lack of resources. The article highlights the need for global readiness and funding to combat the outbreak effectively, as the DRC struggles with its response in contrast to Rwanda's successful handling of a Marburg virus outbreak in 2024. The article is a syndicated summary, and the full original article body could not be extracted.
